# Tail batch — 2026-09-06

Weekly candidate sweep run under [`docs/workflows/discover-candidates.md`](../workflows/discover-candidates.md).
Label: `tail-batch`. Nothing here is scored; a registry row is a candidate, not a commitment to publish.

## Window and scope

**Window swept:** 2026-09-06 (single day). GitHub repository state as of that date; the retrieval
filter `pushed:>=2025-09-06` bounds the sweep to repositories with activity in the preceding twelve months.

**Categories swept — the six thinnest in the taxonomy by head-roster size.** "Thin" is defined here as
the bottom third of the eighteen categories ranked by the number of scored head products, computed at run
time from `sources/categories/*.yaml` after loading the taxonomy through `build.taxonomy.category_statuses()`.
All eighteen categories are `published`, so every row emitted here is promoted later through `add-product`,
not `promote-category`.

| Category | Head products | Registry rows before | Rows added |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `edge_hardware` | 20 | 0 | 12 |
| `dataset_processing_tools` | 21 | 0 | 20 |
| `ml_frameworks` | 25 | 0 | 24 |
| `safeguards` | 26 | 0 | 13 |
| `deployment` | 27 | 0 | 14 |
| `evaluation_code` | 31 | 0 | 10 |

## Predeclared retrieval cutoff

Fixed before the first request, and reported here because it bounds coverage rather than judging any
candidate:

- GitHub repository search, `sort=stars&order=desc`, `per_page=30` — **top 30 per query**
- **star floor 150**, **`pushed:>=2025-09-06`**
- **68 queries**, listed in full below
- For `edge_hardware`, GitHub search is the wrong instrument (the products are boards and chips, not
  repositories), so that category was swept from vendor product pages instead; each candidate's page was
  fetched and its title read to confirm the page names the product.

**The first pass exceeded review capacity.** 591 unique candidates off GitHub search is more than a person can review, which
[`discover-candidates.md`](../workflows/discover-candidates.md#stop-and-escalate-when) says to fix with a
disclosed, tightened retrieval cutoff. That cutoff — **1,000 stars** — was declared before triage began.
Every unique GitHub candidate at or above it (251) was triaged individually. Every unique GitHub candidate
below it (340) is **parked with the cutoff as its stated reason and its source URL recorded**, not dropped: it comes
back in the next sweep and is triaged then. The cutoff bounds how much was retrieved; it says nothing about
whether any candidate is a real product, and it was not used to reject anything the sweep surfaced. The
cutoff applies to the GitHub half only: the 19 `edge_hardware` vendor-page candidates were all triaged
individually, since there is no star count to rank them by and the volume did not need bounding.

**Coverage limitation this creates:** a genuinely new product with fewer than 1,000 GitHub stars that this
sweep surfaced is recorded but not yet judged. 340 candidates are in that state.

## The five reconciled counts

```
raw_signals        = 840
duplicate_signals  = 230
unique_candidates  = 610
accepted           = 93
parked             = 517

raw_signals       = duplicate_signals + unique_candidates   ->  840 = 230 + 610   OK
unique_candidates = accepted          + parked              ->  610 = 93 + 517   OK
```

`raw_signals` counts every result row returned by every query (821 from GitHub search across 68 queries,
19 from vendor-page research for `edge_hardware`). A repository returned by three queries is three raw
signals and one unique candidate; the self-dedup that collapses them is counted in `duplicate_signals`.

Breakdown of `duplicate_signals` by which dedup set caught it:

| Dedup set | Signals caught |
|---|---:|
| head product | 89 |
| existing registry row | 2 |
| resolution ledger | 15 |
| self-dedup (same repo, another query) | 124 |
| **total** | **230** |

Breakdown of `parked`:

| Parked because | Count |
|---|---:|
| triaged individually and not accepted (reason per candidate below) | 170 |
| below the tightened retrieval cutoff of 1,000 stars, carried to the next sweep | 340 |
| `edge_hardware` vendor-page candidates not accepted (reason per candidate below) | 7 |
| **total** | **517** |

## Notes for the reviewer

- **No legitimacy rule was invented during the sweep.** The only threshold applied is the retrieval cutoff,
  declared before triage and reported above; it bounds retrieval and rejects nothing.
- **The warehouse discovery pool (`currentai.entities.repos`) was not reachable from this run**, so dedup ran
  against sets 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 only (head slugs, retired aliases, existing registry rows, the resolution
  ledger, and the candidate set against itself). Pool enrichment — folding several signals into one entity and
  recovering canonical `owner/repo` — did not happen. Presence in the pool never disqualifies a candidate, so
  the missing step costs consolidation, not correctness; a candidate that the pool would have folded into an
  existing entity may still be sitting in the accepted list.
- **Renames are the residual identity risk.** Artifact dedup catches a repository that a head product already
  declares, and a name-fold check was run over head slugs, retired aliases and existing registry slugs on top
  of it. What neither catches is a product on the map whose repository has since moved to an owner or name
  this sweep saw fresh. `vibrantlabsai/ragas` and `NVIDIA-NeMo/Guardrails` were caught this way and deduped;
  another could have slipped.
- **Two candidates were parked as ambiguous rather than guessed:** `unrealcv/unrealcv` (simulator connector or
  dataset tool) and `FedML-AI/FedML` (ml_frameworks or deployment). The Seeed reComputer J4012 is the third —
  its own product page describes it as an NVIDIA Jetson Orin NX 16GB device, and whether that makes it a
  distinct product or a packaging of head product `nvidia-jetson-orin-nx` is not settled by the evidence.
- **No `category-proposal` issue was opened, but two clusters want one:** LLM API gateways (Kong, Portkey,
  bifrost, plano, llmgateway, GoModel, envoy ai-gateway, semantic-router — eight candidates in this batch
  alone) and agent-skill/prompt content collections. Neither has a home in the taxonomy, and both were parked
  on that basis rather than stretched into `deployment` or `safeguards`.
- **A rate limit was hit and retried, never recorded as an absence.** The unauthenticated GitHub search
  ceiling is ten requests a minute; the sweep paced itself under it and retried transient 403/429/5xx.
